# Galaxy Evolution — Research proposals on outflows and feedback regimes

> This page presents three proposal-style research programmes on outflow fate, jet coupling, and the transition from stellar-feedback to AGN-linked regulation. The proposals are framed around measurable observables, matched denominators, and explicit decision criteria.

**AAS pilot PDFs.** Actual-data SDSS DR17 pilot manuscripts are available for each proposal:

- P1: [SDSS high-excitation AGN denominator for outflow escape tests: an SDSS DR17 pilot (PDF)](m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling_aas.pdf)
- P2: [Environment proxy for optical AGN in massive SDSS hosts: an SDSS DR17 pilot (PDF)](m2_p2_radio_jet_environment_aas.pdf)
- P3: [SDSS mass transition in quenching and optical AGN incidence: an SDSS DR17 pilot (PDF)](m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass_aas.pdf)

3 proposal-style research programmes.

## P1 — Escape versus recycling: the fate of AGN-driven multiphase outflows

**Hypothesis / objective.** A substantial fraction of AGN-driven outflowing gas remains gravitationally bound and recycles through the circumgalactic medium, limiting the permanence of feedback-driven gas removal.

**Prior evidence and constraints.**
- Quasar observations show AGN feedback acting on molecular gas reservoirs at high redshift, but such detections do not determine whether gas escapes the halo. [quasar molecular-gas reservoirs](https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.08987).
- Stellar-feedback work on baryon deficiency in low-mass galaxies provides a boundary condition for non-AGN removal channels that must not be confused with AGN-driven escape. [stellar feedback and baryon deficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05584).
- Simulated galaxy feedback constraints highlight that gas removal, recycling, and observable baryon fractions depend on halo scale. [simulated observable feedback constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/0901.1880).

**Remaining uncertainty.** The escaped fraction of multiphase outflows is unmeasured across a representative mass/redshift sample; an outflow detection is not equivalent to permanent quenching.

**Survey/data plan.** AGN hosts and inactive controls spanning stellar mass and redshift; MUSE/MaNGA ionized-gas kinematics, ALMA CO and [C II] for cold gas, JWST/NIRSpec for z>2 outflow tracers, and CGM absorption where available to track recycling.

**Analysis/test and decision criterion.** Compare outflow velocity distributions with halo escape velocities inferred from dynamical/halo-mass estimates, then model escaped versus bound/recycling fractions as a function of stellar mass, halo mass, and redshift. A high escaped fraction at fixed denominator supports permanent-removal feedback; a low escaped fraction supports recycling-limited regulation.

**Limitations and wording guardrails.** Projection, phase conversion, and uncertain halo potentials can dominate the escaped-fraction error budget.

## P2 — Environmental dependence of radio-jet coupling efficiency in galaxy gas

**Hypothesis / objective.** The fraction of radio-jet mechanical power deposited into the multiphase interstellar or circumgalactic medium varies systematically with ambient gas density and host environment.

**Prior evidence and constraints.**
- Young radio galaxies show AGN-driven outflows and provide an empirical route to estimating feedback efficiency. [young radio galaxies and feedback efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.11175).
- Local M51-scale NOEMA data show how jet/AGN activity can affect molecular ISM structure at high spatial resolution, motivating a broader population test. [SWAN IV molecular ISM feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15438).
- Galaxy-group feedback studies identify the group regime as a strong testbed for coupling between radio activity and hot gas. [group-scale AGN feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17145).

**Remaining uncertainty.** Radio luminosity is not a direct coupling-efficiency measurement; the key unknown is the distribution of deposited energy relative to jet power across host gas conditions.

**Survey/data plan.** VLA/LOFAR/MeerKAT for jet morphology and radio spectral ages; Chandra for cavities/shocks/hot gas; MUSE/MaNGA for ionized-gas shock diagnostics; ALMA CO for molecular disturbances; environment from group catalogues.

**Analysis/test and decision criterion.** Estimate jet power and gas work/thermal response in the same apertures, then regress coupling efficiency on gas density, environment, and jet age. A robust environmental dependence requires efficiency differences larger than radio-power calibration uncertainties.

**Limitations and wording guardrails.** Radio-to-jet-power conversions are uncertain; coupling estimates must be reported with systematic floors.

## P3 — Locating the transition from stellar-feedback to AGN-feedback regulation

**Hypothesis / objective.** There is a stellar/halo-mass regime where stellar-feedback momentum and energy budgets cease to explain gas loss or quiescence, and AGN-linked observables become necessary to reproduce the measured quenched fraction and baryon deficit.

**Prior evidence and constraints.**
- Low-mass baryon deficiency attributed to stellar feedback provides the low-mass endpoint of the transition problem. [stellar-feedback baryon deficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05584).
- High-redshift environmental-quenching simulations emphasize that quenching mechanisms vary with mass and redshift, not only with local observables. [high-redshift environmental quenching simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03008).
- Simulated observable-property constraints show why feedback-regime boundaries must be tested against measured baryonic properties. [feedback constraints in simulated galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/0901.1880).

**Remaining uncertainty.** The observational crossover mass between stellar-feedback-dominated regulation and AGN-linked regulation is not cleanly measured with the same denominator and gas observables.

**Survey/data plan.** DESI/GAMA/COSMOS parent samples with stellar mass and halo proxy; ALFALFA/FASHI HI and ALMA CO gas fractions; optical/IR SFRs; X-ray/radio AGN indicators; JWST for high-redshift extension.

**Analysis/test and decision criterion.** Fit a broken-slope or transition model for gas fraction, baryon deficit, and quenched fraction as functions of stellar/halo mass while including AGN incidence and stellar-feedback proxies. The transition is credible only if the AGN-linked term improves predictive power above the stellar-feedback model at high mass.

**Limitations and wording guardrails.** Halo-mass uncertainties and selection functions can smear the transition; results should be framed as a probability surface, not a single universal mass.

## Methodological note

- Evidence-traceability work remains useful as methodology support, but it is not presented here as an astrophysical proposal because it does not test a physical galaxy-evolution hypothesis.

## Scope note

These are proposed research designs, not accepted claims. Source links in the proposal text identify prior evidence or constraints that motivate the study; they do not by themselves prove the proposed hypothesis. Each proposal is written to be testable through future survey analysis, matched controls, and explicit decision criteria.

===== 01_m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr
13:We present a selection-aware matched-control comparison of catalog specific star-formation rates (sSFRs) in broad BPT optical AGN hosts and star-forming controls drawn from the SDSS DR17 spectroscopic catalog. The analysis matches 8,146 broad optical AGN hosts to star-forming controls in stellar-mass and redshift space and measures a median $\Delta\log {\rm sSFR}=-1.309$ dex. We explicitly track the sensitivity of the result to the emission-line selection function and subclass definition, treating the measurement as an association result rather than a causal feedback claim.
19:While characterizing causal feedback typically requires multi-wavelength data, establishing a rigorous optical baseline is an essential first step. Here we present a selection-aware matched-control pilot analyzing catalog specific star-formation rates (sSFRs) in broad BPT optical AGN hosts and star-forming controls drawn from the SDSS DR17 spectroscopic catalog. Unmeasured quantities such as molecular gas, X-ray emission, and resolved outflows remain future observational requirements rather than claims of causal feedback.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.
74:\caption{Matched-pair catalog-sSFR offsets for broad BPT optical AGN hosts minus nearest star-forming controls in stellar-mass--redshift space. The large negative offset is robust within the optical emission-line subset but remains selection- and subclass-dependent.}
79:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
83:In the SDSS DR17 emission-line subset, broad BPT optical AGN hosts show a median sSFR offset of $-1.309$ dex relative to mass--redshift matched controls. The offset remains large but decreases to $-0.744$ dex at S/N$\geq 10$, with the 95\% bootstrap interval staying entirely negative, so the result should be read as a selection-dependent optical association rather than evidence for causal AGN quenching.
===== 02_m1_rp2_environment_quenching
13:We use a representative 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog to build an optical density-proxy analysis of environmental quenching. A 10th-nearest-neighbor density proxy is compared with quenched fraction after controlling for stellar mass and redshift; the high-density quartile has quenched fraction 0.230 versus 0.181 in the low-density quartile. This analysis is intentionally limited to the optical denominator and treats the missing group and halo information as a future-data requirement.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.
74:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
===== 03_m1_rp3_maintenance_heating
13:We use a 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog to construct an optical denominator for maintenance-heating follow-up in massive galaxies. Among massive, low-sSFR hosts, we measure the available BPT-AGN fraction as a proxy for the duty-cycle denominator relevant to future X-ray or radio maintenance-heating studies. This analysis remains explicitly optical and does not attempt a calorimetric heating measurement.
19:Maintenance-heating interpretations require X-ray or radio observables, but a rigorous optical denominator is a necessary first step. Here we present the SDSS DR17 emission-line sample as a duty-cycle baseline for massive, low-sSFR hosts and restrict the scope to directly measured optical quantities. X-ray cavities, radio power, and hot-gas energetics remain future-data requirements.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.
56:\item This provides an optical duty-cycle denominator for X-ray/radio maintenance-heating follow-up, not a heating-to-cooling measurement.
63:\caption{SDSS DR17 optical denominator/proxy diagnostic for maintenance-heating follow-up. The figure summarizes the cached optical result used for target definition or denominator design.}
70:Radio-mode and hot-atmosphere studies define the future calorimetric observables--jet power, cavities, cooling luminosity, and group gas--that are absent from this optical denominator \citep{best2005,mcnamara2007,mcnamara2012,heckmanbest2014,eckert2024}.
73:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
77:The massive subset contains 9,298 emission-line galaxies, with 5,695 classified as low-sSFR. The BPT AGN fraction rises from 0.430 in the massive subset to 0.607 in the massive low-sSFR subset, defining an optical duty-cycle denominator for maintenance-heating follow-up rather than a direct heating result.
===== 04_m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling
4:\shorttitle{SDSS high-excitation AGN denominator for outflow escape tests}
8:\title{SDSS high-excitation AGN denominator for outflow escape tests}
13:We use a 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog to define the optical denominator for an outflow escape-versus-recycling program. The analysis counts high-excitation optical AGN candidates and records their median sSFR as a proxy for where resolved kinematics and multiphase-gas follow-up should focus. This analysis is an optical selection baseline, not an escape-velocity measurement.
19:An optical line-ratio selection can define a useful target set for future outflow work, but it cannot measure escape or recycling on its own. Here we present the SDSS DR17 emission-line sample as an optical baseline for massive galaxies and restrict the scope to directly measured quantities. Resolved kinematics, halo potentials, and multiphase gas remain future-data requirements.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.
51:This note quantifies the high-excitation optical-AGN denominator needed for future resolved-kinematics tests of escape versus recycling. The result is an optical baseline rather than a direct escape-velocity measurement.
54:\item High-excitation optical AGN candidates number 4,440 of 60,000 emission-line galaxies (0.074).
63:\caption{SDSS DR17 optical denominator/proxy diagnostic for outflow escape-versus-recycling follow-up. The figure summarizes the cached optical result used for target definition.}
73:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
77:High-excitation optical AGN candidates number 4,440 of 60,000 emission-line galaxies, and their median log sSFR is -11.53 compared with -10.14 for the full denominator. The optical sample therefore defines a follow-up denominator for resolved escape/recycling work, but SDSS alone cannot measure outflow velocity or fate.
===== 05_m2_p2_radio_jet_environment
13:We build an optical denominator for radio-jet environment follow-up using a 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog. In massive hosts, we compare the high- and low-density quartiles of the optical AGN fraction to identify an environment-stratified target set for later radio or X-ray work. The result is an optical baseline only; it does not measure jet power or coupling efficiency.
19:Radio-jet environment studies require radio and X-ray data, but an optical denominator is a useful starting point. Here we present the SDSS DR17 emission-line sample as an environment-stratified baseline for massive hosts and restrict the analysis to directly measured optical quantities. Jet power, coupling efficiency, and hot-gas structure remain future-data requirements.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.

73:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
77:Among massive hosts, the optical AGN fraction is 0.509 in the high-density quartile and 0.367 in the low-density quartile, with a bootstrap difference of [0.112, 0.170]. This establishes an environment-stratified optical denominator for radio-jet coupling studies, not a direct coupling measurement.
===== 06_m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass
19:The stellar-mass scale where quenched fraction and optical AGN incidence rise together is useful as an empirical transition marker, but it is not by itself a physical-feedback measurement. Here we present the SDSS DR17 emission-line sample as an optical transition baseline and restrict the scope to directly measured quantities. Gas fractions, baryon deficits, and halo-scale measurements remain future-data requirements.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. ===== 07_m3_p1_multiphase_census
13:We build a common optical denominator for a multiphase outflow census from a 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog. Simple tracer definitions change the inferred AGN or feedback-candidate prevalence within the same denominator, so the draft focuses on the optical selection baseline needed before adding ionized, neutral, molecular, or X-ray/radio tracers. This is a denominator study, not a multiphase outflow measurement.
19:Multiphase outflow work requires ionized, neutral, molecular, and X-ray or radio measurements, but a common optical denominator is a practical starting point. Here we present the SDSS DR17 emission-line sample as that baseline and restrict the manuscript to directly measured optical quantities. The missing multiphase observables remain future-data requirements.
43:Cached-versus-public marginal checks found no redshift, stellar-mass, or sSFR bin with a cached-minus-public fraction difference above 5 percentage points. The largest absolute differences were 2.03 percentage points in redshift, -1.63 percentage points in stellar mass, and -0.58 percentage points in sSFR. This is a representativeness diagnostic only; it does not make the capped cache random or complete.
56:\item This demonstrates why a common-denominator multiphase census is required; it does not measure molecular or neutral outflow rates.
63:\caption{SDSS DR17 optical denominator/proxy diagnostic for the common-denominator multiphase census. The figure summarizes the cached optical result used for target definition.}
70:A real multiphase census needs independent ionized, neutral, molecular, and energetic outflow observables; the present SDSS thresholds are an optical denominator only \citep{veilleux2005,rupke2018,cicone2014,fiore2017,feruglio2015,woo2016,bae2018}.
73:\section{Data Availability}\label{sec:data-avail}
77:Within the 60,000-galaxy denominator, simple tracer definitions change prevalence from 0.136 to 0.418, a factor of 3.1. That spread shows why a common-denominator census is required, while also underscoring that the present SDSS sample cannot measure molecular, neutral, or X-ray/radio outflow phases.
===== 08_m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency
19:Gas-fraction and star-formation-efficiency interpretations require CO or dust data, but the optical denominator is the necessary starting point. **Ranked findings**

- **Major** - [/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/02_m1_rp2_environment_quenching/aastex/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex:13) overclaims representativeness in the abstract. The manuscript later says the subset is a capped, `specObjID`-ordered 60,000-row sample that is “reproducible but not random or population-complete” ([line 23](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/02_m1_rp2_environment_quenching/aastex/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex:23)). The word `representative` should be removed.
- **Major** - [/m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/04_m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling/aastex/m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling_integrated.tex:54), [/m2_p2_radio_jet_environment_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/05_m2_p2_radio_jet_environment/aastex/m2_p2_radio_jet_environment_integrated.tex:54), [/m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/08_m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency/aastex/m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency_integrated.tex:54), and [/m3_p3_simulation_validation_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/09_m3_p3_simulation_validation/aastex/m3_p3_simulation_validation_integrated.tex:54) do not fully define the key denominator/selection criteria in the main text. The counts are real, but the papers should expose the exact threshold definitions in prose or a table note so the result is reproducible without the analysis script.
- **Major** - [/m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709D163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/06_m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass/aastex/m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass_integrated.tex:54) treats the highest-mass bin as a resolved “transition mass.” That bin is the open upper tail `11.0--12.5`, so the result is better framed as a binned upper-mass transition diagnostic, not a single transition scale.
- **Minor** - [/m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709Z163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/01_m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr/aastex/m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr_integrated.tex:13) uses `broad BPT optical AGN hosts`, which can be misread as broad-line AGN. `BPT-selected optical AGN hosts` is clearer and keeps the class definition aligned with the analysis.
- **Minor** - All nine manuscripts still use `\affiliation{Public SDSS DR17 data only}`. That is a provenance note, not an affiliation. Move that wording into `\dataavailability`, a footnote, or a manuscript note so the affiliation field stays institutional.
- **Improvement** - The `Data Availability` section is generic in every paper and does not give a repo-relative path for the local subset/manifest. That is acceptable for internal drafting, but the pilot would benefit from a concrete path note to the cached CSV/manifest used for each paper.
- **Improvement** - Papers 02 through 09 all reuse the generic `fig-topic.pdf` filename and similarly templated captions. That is fragile for future rebuilds because the caption alone has to carry identity. Paper-specific filenames and a one-line metric in the caption would reduce cross-wire risk.

**Exact feed for the PDF-writing pilot**

- **For 02**: rewrite the abstract first sentence to “We use a capped 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog…” and add a caveat sentence that the sample is `specObjID`-ordered and not random or population-complete. Keep `0.230` and `0.181` unchanged.
- **For 04**: insert the exact high-excitation selection rule in the first results sentence or a table note. Safe pattern: “High-excitation optical AGN candidates, defined by [exact BPT/line-ratio rule from the analysis script], number 4,440 of 60,000 emission-line galaxies.” Do not leave the criterion implicit.
- **For 05**: define “massive hosts” in text, not only in the analysis pipeline. Safe pattern: “Among massive hosts, defined as [exact mass cut used in the analysis], …” Then keep the `0.509` and `0.367` fractions exactly as written.
- **For 08**: define the mass and sSFR thresholds that produce the 6,729-galaxy denominator. Safe pattern: “Among galaxies selected by [exact mass/sSFR cuts], we measure an optical BPT AGN fraction of `0.549 ± 0.006` and a median Hα proxy of `40.06`.” Do not infer thresholds; move the actual analysis-script cuts into the manuscript.
- **For 09**: add a compact binning note or table footnote with the exact cell grid:
  - Mass bins: `8.0-9.5`, `9.5-10.0`, `10.0-10.5`, `10.5-11.0`, `11.0-12.5`
  - Redshift bins: `0.02-0.05`, `0.05-0.08`, `0.08-0.12`
  - Reporting rule: only cells with `n >= 50`
  - Then say explicitly that this yields 15 cells.
- **For 06**: replace “the first stellar-mass bin with quenched fraction above 0.5 is `11.0--12.5`” with “the highest-mass bin in the current binning, `11.0--12.5`, is the first bin with quenched fraction above 0.5.” In the conclusion, call it a “binned upper-mass transition diagnostic,” not a single transition mass.
- **For 01**: change “broad BPT optical AGN hosts” to “BPT-selected optical AGN hosts” everywhere the class appears.
- **For all papers**: keep the measured values exactly as in the source-analysis JSONs, and keep the explicit non-causality language. Do not strengthen “baseline,” “denominator,” or “proxy” language into physical-mechanism claims.

**Real-data/source/citation audit notes**

- The numeric claims in the manuscripts match the local source-analysis JSONs.
  - 01: `8,146` matched pairs, median `Δlog sSFR = -1.308887`, bootstrap interval `[-1.33413855, -1.28213994]`.
  - 02: high-density quenched fraction `0.2304` (`3456/15000`) vs low-density `0.180667` (`2710/15000`), high-density coefficient `0.032495`.
  - 03: massive subset `9,298`, low-sSFR subset `5,695`, AGN fractions `0.430` and `0.607`.
  - 04: high-excitation count `4,440/60,000`, median `log sSFR = -11.53205`.
  - 05: high-density massive AGN fraction `0.508584` vs low-density `0.366715`.
  - 06: mass-bin arrays and transition logic are real; the issue is wording, not the values.
  - 07: prevalence span `0.136` to `0.418`.
  - 08: denominator `6,729`, AGN fraction `0.548670`, median Hα proxy `40.061174`.
  - 09: 15 mass-redshift cells are real; the manuscript just needs the grid exposed explicitly.
- No mock, synthetic, placeholder, or toy data were used as evidence in the inspected paths.
- The public-linked research-topic page is explicit that the linked PDFs are proposal-style outputs and that source links motivate, but do not prove, the hypotheses.
- I did not find a citation typo like the earlier `Dubrois` issue in the current cycle 07 TeX; the `Dubois` references in 09 are spelled correctly.
- The public-linked PDFs are identical between the local public root and the live root by sha256, so there is no public/live drift in the inspected surface.

**Workflow/system notes**

- All nine cycle 07 PDFs compile cleanly and the receipt reports no fatal markers.
- The public-linked research-topic packet is an older snapshot relative to the cycle 07 candidate package, so if the intent is publication parity, the pipeline should include an explicit refresh step from the candidate-copy state rather than relying on the public folder as a source of truth.
- The current page structure is mechanically consistent, but the repeated generic figure naming and non-standard affiliation note are maintenance risks for the pilot.
- The `ROLE_TABLE_BLOCKER` / `NO ACTIVE EXECUTION PHRASE` markers on the method2 public page indicate that the wiki workflow still has a separate governance path. Keep the candidate-copy PDF-writing pilot isolated from that path.

- **Major** - [/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/02_m1_rp2_environment_quenching/aastex/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex:13) overclaims representativeness in the abstract. The manuscript later says the subset is a capped, `specObjID`-ordered 60,000-row sample that is “reproducible but not random or population-complete” ([line 23](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/02_m1_rp2_environment_quenching/aastex/m1_rp2_environment_quenching_integrated.tex:23)). The word `representative` should be removed.
- **Major** - [/m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/04_m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling/aastex/m2_p1_outflow_escape_recycling_integrated.tex:54), [/m2_p2_radio_jet_environment_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/05_m2_p2_radio_jet_environment/aastex/m2_p2_radio_jet_environment_integrated.tex:54), [/m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/08_m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency/aastex/m3_p2_gas_depletion_efficiency_integrated.tex:54), and [/m3_p3_simulation_validation_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709T163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/09_m3_p3_simulation_validation/aastex/m3_p3_simulation_validation_integrated.tex:54) do not fully define the key denominator/selection criteria in the main text. The counts are real, but the papers should expose the exact threshold definitions in prose or a table note so the result is reproducible without the analysis script.
- **Major** - [/m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709D163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/06_m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass/aastex/m2_p3_feedback_transition_mass_integrated.tex:54) treats the highest-mass bin as a resolved “transition mass.” That bin is the open upper tail `11.0--12.5`, so the result is better framed as a binned upper-mass transition diagnostic, not a single transition scale.
- **Minor** - [/m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr_integrated.tex](/Users/duhokim/NebulaMind/NebulaMind/.hermes/handoffs/galaxy-evolution/mastermind/aas-autopilot/overnight-critical-review/OVERNIGHT_PDF_REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SWARM_20260709Z163102Z/candidates/cycle_07_nine_papers/01_m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr/aastex/m1_rp1_sdss_agn_sfr_integrated.tex:13) uses `broad BPT optical AGN hosts`, which can be misread as broad-line AGN. `BPT-selected optical AGN hosts` is clearer and keeps the class definition aligned with the analysis.
- **Minor** - All nine manuscripts still use `\affiliation{Public SDSS DR17 data only}`. That is a provenance note, not an affiliation. Move that wording into `\dataavailability`, a footnote, or a manuscript note so the affiliation field stays institutional.
- **Improvement** - The `Data Availability` section is generic in every paper and does not give a repo-relative path for the local subset/manifest. That is acceptable for internal drafting, but the pilot would benefit from a concrete path note to the cached CSV/manifest used for each paper.
- **Improvement** - Papers 02 through 09 all reuse the generic `fig-topic.pdf` filename and similarly templated captions. That is fragile for future rebuilds because the caption alone has to carry identity. Paper-specific filenames and a one-line metric in the caption would reduce cross-wire risk.

**Exact feed for the PDF-writing pilot**

- **For 02**: rewrite the abstract first sentence to “We use a capped 60,000-galaxy subset of the SDSS DR17 emission-line catalog…” and add a caveat sentence that the sample is `specObjID`-ordered and not random or population-complete. Keep `0.230` and `0.181` unchanged.
- **For 04**: insert the exact high-excitation selection rule in the first results sentence or a table note. Safe pattern: “High-excitation optical AGN candidates, defined by [exact BPT/line-ratio rule from the analysis script], number 4,440 of 60,000 emission-line galaxies.” Do not leave the criterion implicit.
- **For 05**: define “massive hosts” in text, not only in the analysis pipeline. Safe pattern: “Among massive hosts, defined as [exact mass cut used in the analysis], …” Then keep the `0.509` and `0.367` fractions exactly as written.
- **For 08**: define the mass and sSFR thresholds that produce the 6,729-galaxy denominator. Safe pattern: “Among galaxies selected by [exact mass/sSFR cuts], we measure an optical BPT AGN fraction of `0.549 ± 0.006` and a median Hα proxy of `40.06`.” Do not infer thresholds; move the actual analysis-script cuts into the manuscript.
- **For 09**: add a compact binning note or table footnote with the exact cell grid:
  - Mass bins: `8.0-9.5`, `9.5-10.0`, `10.0-10.5`, `10.5-11.0`, `11.0-12.5`
  - Redshift bins: `0.02-0.05`, `0.05-0.08`, `0.08-0.12`
  - Reporting rule: only cells with `n >= 50`
  - Then say explicitly that this yields 15 cells.
- **For 06**: replace “the first stellar-mass bin with quenched fraction above 0.5 is `11.0--12.5`” with “the highest-mass bin in the current binning, `11.0--12.5`, is the first bin with quenched fraction above 0.5.” In the conclusion, call it a “binned upper-mass transition diagnostic,” not a single transition mass.
- **For 01**: change “broad BPT optical AGN hosts” to “BPT-selected optical AGN hosts” everywhere the class appears.
- **For all papers**: keep the measured values exactly as in the source-analysis JSONs, and keep the explicit non-causality language. Do not strengthen “baseline,” “denominator,” or “proxy” language into physical-mechanism claims.

**Real-data/source/citation audit notes**

- The numeric claims in the manuscripts match the local source-analysis JSONs.
  - 01: `8,146` matched pairs, median `Δlog sSFR = -1.308887`, bootstrap interval `[-1.33413855, -1.28213994]`.

  - 08: denominator `6,729`, AGN fraction `0.548670`, median Hα proxy `40.061174`.
